# AI-DLC Audit Log

## Workflow Start
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-17T11:50:16Z
**Event**: WORKFLOW_STARTED
**Scope**: meeting-card-scheduler
**Request**: /aidlc Initiate a full-stack local web application for a Meeting Scheduler & Invitation Card Generator with zero third-party API dependencies.\n\nScope & Core Features:\n1. Meeting Scheduling & Details:\n   - Form inputs: Meeting title, purpose/description, date, start time, end time/duration, timezone selector, meeting link (or physical location), and host profile (name, role/organization, contact email).\n   - Dynamic Agenda Builder: Add, edit, reorder, and remove itemized agenda topics with duration estimates and designated speaker names.\n   - Attendee Management: Input guest list (name, email, role/designation).\n\n2. Invitation Card Engine (PDF Export):\n   - Client-side or embedded local PDF generation (e.g., using @react-pdf/renderer or pdfkit). Strictly NO external SaaS APIs or microservices.\n   - Aesthetic Card Layout: Formatted as a sleek event card/invitation badge (dimensions: standard A5 or US Letter card).\n   - Visual Details: Clean typography, visual hierarchy, agenda timeline block, host details badge, and an auto-generated QR code (containing the meeting URL or .ics calendar payload).\n   - Live Preview: Split-screen or modal showing real-time card preview before downloading the PDF.\n\n3. Architecture & Constraints:\n   - Stack: Next.js (App Router), TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, and Lucide icons.\n   - Storage: Local browser persistence (IndexedDB / LocalStorage) or local SQLite with zero cloud database dependency.\n   - Portability: Single self-contained repository with standard npm run dev and npm run build workflows.\n\nLifecycle Guidance:\n- Run the Inception phase: Document requirements, edge cases, and component architecture inside aidlc-docs/.\n- Formulate an execution plan with discrete units of work and present the approval gate before generating code.
**Source Baseline**: sha256:5bf349c8e337bcb359919c7be1b77ee367dae33ba7f1523289e0749b7c601482

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:12:15Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-requirements
**Details**: Q2: The zero-third-party-API constraint extends to observability tooling. Rely on the hosting platform's built-in logs/console output and Next.js error boundaries only — no external error-tracking SaaS.

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:12:19Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: nfr-requirements
**Decision**: Reliability target (uptime SLO) and data-loss/backup posture for browser-only storage
**Options**: Best-effort uptime + no backup/export (accept data-loss risk),Best-effort uptime + flag export/import backup as a future Should-Have,Formal uptime SLO required + no backup,Formal uptime SLO required + flag export/import backup as future Should-Have

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:12:32Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-requirements
**Details**: Q3: Best-effort uptime, no formal SLA, for this internal small-team tool. Browser-data loss (user clears storage or switches browsers) is an accepted, undocumented risk — no export/import backup feature.

---

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:12:35Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED

**Decision**: Security posture for the unlisted, unauthenticated hosted URL: HTTP security headers and rate-limiting scope
**Options**: Mandate standard security headers only (CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options/X-Content-Type-Options), no rate-limiting,Mandate security headers plus basic rate-limiting/bot mitigation,No additional mandate beyond the already-affirmed scanning gates

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:12:46Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-requirements
**Details**: Q4: Mandate standard HTTP security headers (CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options). No rate-limiting/bot mitigation — the unlisted URL plus already-affirmed scanning gates are the primary controls for this low-stakes internal tool.

## Decision Recorded

**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED

**Decision**: Expected concurrent-user ceiling for the small team, to set the load assumption for performance/scalability targets
**Options**: Under 20 concurrent users - no auto-scaling/load-balancing needed,20-100 concurrent users - basic auto-scaling recommended,100+ concurrent users - full scalability design needed

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:12:59Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-requirements
**Details**: Q5: Design for under 20 concurrent users, matching a small internal team. No auto-scaling or load-balancing NFR needed — a single serverless/static instance comfortably covers this load.

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:19Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Decision**: Should resilience design skip circuit-breaker/retry/bulkhead patterns entirely (no external calls) and focus only on the persistence/card-generation graceful-degradation mechanism?
**Options**: Yes - skip circuit-breaker/retry/bulkhead patterns entirely,No - design a generic resilience pattern anyway for future-proofing

---

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:26Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN
**Session**: f784b51a-745b-4c44-a68b-d9c790031786

---

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:29Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Details**: Q1: Skip circuit-breaker/retry-with-backoff/bulkhead patterns entirely — nothing external exists to protect against. reliability-design.md focuses solely on the concrete persistence and card-generation graceful-degradation mechanism (NFR4.1-NFR4.3).

---

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:32Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Decision**: Caching architecture: is any custom caching design needed beyond Vercel's default CDN static-asset caching?
**Options**: No custom caching layer - Vercel default CDN caching is sufficient,Design an explicit custom caching strategy

---

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:47Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN
**Session**: f784b51a-745b-4c44-a68b-d9c790031786

---

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:51Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Details**: Q2: No custom caching layer. performance-design.md documents that Vercel's default CDN static-asset caching is sufficient — no per-user server-side data exists to cache.

---

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:29:54Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Decision**: Should logical-components.md map failure domains directly onto the four existing domain-design components (Presentation, Scheduling, PersistenceAdapter, CardGeneration) as in-process isolation boundaries?
**Options**: Yes - map onto the four existing components as in-process error-boundary domains,No - define a different failure-domain breakdown

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-18T11:30:02Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Details**: Q3: Map failure domains onto the four existing domain-design components (Presentation, Scheduling, PersistenceAdapter, CardGeneration) as in-process isolation boundaries — e.g. a CardGeneration failure is isolated by a React error boundary, not a service/network boundary.

**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: nfr-design
**Decision**: Should CSRF protection be marked explicitly out of scope in security-design.md, since there's no session/cookie auth and no server-side mutating endpoint?
**Options**: Yes - mark CSRF out of scope with rationale,No - design a CSRF mechanism anyway

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T03:59:33Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Decision**: Infrastructure-as-Code approach for the single Vercel project: formal IaC tool or checked-in vercel.json plus dashboard-configured project settings?
**Options**: vercel.json + dashboard-configured settings - no formal IaC tool,Formal IaC (Terraform Vercel provider)

---

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T04:01:52Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN
**Session**: f784b51a-745b-4c44-a68b-d9c790031786

---

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T04:01:57Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Details**: Q1: vercel.json plus Vercel's own git-linked dashboard/CLI settings, no formal IaC tool — a single project with nothing to provision doesn't warrant a Terraform/CDK layer.

---

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T04:02:02Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Decision**: Should PR preview deployments (Vercel's automatic per-PR preview) be enabled and relied on as a pre-merge manual smoke-check step?
**Options**: Yes - enable and use PR preview deployments as a manual smoke-check,No - decline preview deployments to keep the pipeline as simple as the no-staging posture implies

---

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T04:03:36Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN
**Session**: f784b51a-745b-4c44-a68b-d9c790031786

---

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T04:03:42Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Details**: Q2: Yes, enable and use PR preview deployments as a manual pre-merge smoke-check — it's free with the git integration already needed for deploy-on-merge and adds no pipeline complexity.

---

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T04:03:47Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Decision**: Should cicd-pipeline.md name Vercel's native immutable-deployment rollback as the sole rollback procedure, with no custom blue-green/canary tooling?
**Options**: Yes - Vercel native rollback only,No - design a custom rollback strategy

---

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T07:20:33Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN
**Session**: f784b51a-745b-4c44-a68b-d9c790031786

---

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T07:20:41Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Details**: Q3: Yes, Vercel's native immutable-deployment rollback (dashboard/CLI) is the sole rollback procedure — no data-migration risk exists to complicate it, so no custom blue-green/canary tooling is needed.

---

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T07:20:44Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Decision**: Does any CI/CD secret need to be provisioned (e.g. a Vercel deploy token for GitHub Actions), or does Vercel's native GitHub App integration handle deploy-on-merge with no secret in GitHub Actions at all?
**Options**: No secret needed - Vercel's native GitHub App integration handles deploy-on-merge,A Vercel deploy token is needed in GitHub Actions secrets

---

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T07:23:38Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN
**Session**: f784b51a-745b-4c44-a68b-d9c790031786

---

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-09-19T07:23:41Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: infrastructure-design
**Details**: Q4: No CI/CD secret is needed. Vercel's native GitHub App integration triggers the production deploy and PR previews directly; GitHub Actions only runs the pre-merge quality/security gates and needs no Vercel credential.
